## 2026-11-02 — run 26: the paragraph of Kant's chapter on phenomena and noumena between B 312 and B 313, the moderns' use of the terms mundus sensibilis and intelligibilis, the sense in which an object is taken as noumenon, the question of a transcendental use of the understanding answered in the negative, and Kant's footnote at B 312 on the terms intellectual and intelligible

Twenty-sixth scheduled run, on `main`, on the configured model `claude-fable-5-1` (the same the
last entry records; no handover). Read all governing files first. The run fired the day after
run 25; `origin/main` stood at run 25's commit when it began, one ahead of the container's
detached checkout, which was fast-forwarded before anything was written; nothing arrived in
between.

**Step 1.** No ask changed status. Asks 4 (deferred), 9 and 10 stay open; none blocks the
plan.

**Step 2.** `INBOX.md` carries the owner notes of 2026-10-11 and 2026-10-19, each handled in
the session that received it; nothing new. No dispute, no attestation, no form filing (the
workflow made no commit).

**Step 3.** No open motion.

**Step 4, the nodes: 5 new (2 passages, 3 claims), 9 revised in their bodies, the first page touched in two rows, its passage list and five places of its prose, no link set.**
The plan's next content item was the gap both the first page and `memory.md` named first since
2026-11-01: the one paragraph of the chapter on phenomena and noumena still in no passage
between B 310 and the chapter's end, the paragraph on the moderns' use of the terms *mundus
sensibilis* and *intelligibilis*, closing with the sentence that the question of a transcendental
use of the understanding reaching the noumenon as an object has been answered in the negative, a
candidate for the row on what can be said. It was taken from korpora.org's Akademie text (AA III
212–213) as one passage whole,
`p-krv-a256-257-b312-313-the-moderns-use-of-mundus-sensibilis-and-intelligibilis-and-the-question-answered-in-the-negative`
(3:212.28–213.21, six sentences by full stop and question mark, the fifth's semicolon joining two
halves), and Kant's footnote, whose mark stands in its first sentence after »intelligibilis,«
(3:212.29), as a second passage on the model of the footnote at B xxvi, `p-krv-b312-footnote`
(two sentences, five printed lines, cited by the page of its mark). The 1781 pages were read
before anything was written, A 257 on wikisource's transcription and A 256 on the scan of the
page itself, after the transcription's catchword »ten« made the page's end suspect: A 256 ends
inside the first sentence's »Alten«, Al-|ten, as A 258 ends inside »Einheit« (run 25), and A 257
ends with the paragraph at »beantwortet haben«, catchword »Wenn«. The scan settled a second
thing the transcription only suggested: the 1781 page carries neither a footnote mark after
»intelligibilis,« nor any note at its foot, and the transcription of A 257 carries none, so the
map holds the note as the second edition's and says so on both passages, the three nodes that
cite it and the page. The 1781 text differs in no word; five comma variants are listed sentence
by sentence (a comma after »nichts« in the first, after »dagegen« and none after »Welt« in the
third, after »dadurch« in the fourth, the semicolon a comma and a comma after »allein« in the
fifth), and the orthography named and not listed; the transcription marks nothing as emphasized
and sets the Latin terms in antiqua, and the Akademie text sets »mundi sensibilis« and
»intelligibilis« in another face, the tag spaces normalized.

*The three claims, all `draft`, each with its quote.* From the paragraph's fifth sentence,
one claim with its three parts in the text's order: that in regard to appearances understanding
and reason »läßt sich allerdings ... brauchen«; that the question is whether they still have
»einigen Gebrauch« when the object is not appearance, »(Noumenon)«, kept as a question; and that
»in diesem Sinne nimmt man ihn, wenn er an sich als bloß intelligibel, d. i. dem Verstande
allein und gar nicht den Sinnen gegeben, gedacht wird«, the mode »gedacht wird« kept
(`c-kant-object-taken-as-noumenon-when-thought-as-in-itself-merely-intelligible`). From the
sixth, the question »ob außer jenem empirischen Gebrauche des Verstandes ... noch ein
transscendentaler möglich sei, der auf das Noumenon als einen Gegenstand gehe, welche Frage wir
verneinend beantwortet haben«, the »also« dropped, »jenem empirischen Gebrauche« resolved as the
fifth sentence's use in regard to appearances, the text's »wir« kept, and the node saying that
reading a sentence which reports its author's answer as given as asserting that answer is the
map's reading, open to dispute
(`c-kant-question-of-a-transcendental-use-of-the-understanding-reaching-the-noumenon-answered-in-the-negative`).
From the footnote's second sentence, the prescription »müssen (unerachtet der Härte des Lauts)
intelligibel oder sensibel heißen« of what can only be an object of the one or the other kind
of intuition, its »aber« dropped and named, the first sentence's ban on the expression of an
intellectual world and its ground, »denn intellectuell oder sensitiv sind nur die
Erkenntnisse«, in the passage and in no claim and named on the claim
(`c-kant-objects-of-one-or-the-other-kind-of-intuition-must-be-called-intelligible-or-sensible`).
The paragraph's first four sentences, on the moderns' usage, the two astronomies and such a
twisting of words as »eine bloße sophistische Ausflucht«, are in the passage and in no claim:
they speak of a use of words and of what it evades, name no noumenon in the chapter's sense,
and say nothing of what can be said of the thing in itself or of what divides it from
appearance, so fall outside both rows' criteria, said on the passage and in the gaps list.
Terms: »Wortkrämerei« "word-mongering", »Wortverdrehung« "twisting of words", »Ausflucht«
"evasion", »Gemächlichkeit« "convenience", »freilich« "admittedly" kept apart from »allerdings«
"certainly" as at B 311, »Inbegriff« "sum total" as in the B xxvi footnote, »Weltbau« "the
structure of the world", »unerachtet« "notwithstanding", »Härte des Lauts« "harshness of the
sound"; the subjunctives of the indirect question rendered by the indicative as on every Kant
passage; »die erstere ... die zweite« "the former ... the second", the English order the
German.

*The links.* None set. The sixth sentence's claim names where the map holds the answer it
reports, B 308–309's use of the categories reaching in no way beyond the limit of the objects
of experience, and says that this is the answer is the map's reading, drawn by no connective
that names the place, so no `depends_on`; it names, too, that B 314 two paragraphs on leaves
unknown whether a transcendental cognition is possible at all where this sentence has the
question of a transcendental use answered in the negative, the two in one row and not
reconciled by the map, open to dispute. The fifth sentence's claim refuses to identify its
sense of the noumenon with the positive sense B 311 refuses or with B 307's negative sense,
each identification the map's; names A 538's »intelligibel«, of something in an object of the
senses, and the footnote's, of objects by their kind of intuition, as uses the map does not
relate; and names § 19's and § 25's »denkbar« (the sentence says how an object is taken when
thought so, not what is thinkable), § 22's immediate cognition (the divergence on immediacy)
and GD Vernunft 6's fourth proposition (a division of a world, where this names the sense of
one object). The footnote's claim names B 311's division of concepts into sensible and
intellectual, with no passage saying whether »Begriffe« fall under »Erkenntnisse«, and the
other thinkers' »intelligibel«, which names a character or a freedom (§ 55, JGB 36, GD
Irrthümer 8) and never objects by their kind of intuition; a prescription, by the convention,
shares and denies nothing.

*The page.* `x-thing-in-itself`: the sixth sentence's claim appended to the Kant cell of the row
on what can be said, by the criterion of 2026-10-30, for what it says has been denied of the
noumenon; the fifth sentence's and the footnote's appended to the Kant cell of the row on the
division, the row's first question, the one for what its last clause says the object is taken
as when it is not appearance, the other for what it says the division of objects into sensible
and intelligible goes by. Both notes gain their sentences; the two passages join the page's list;
under *What the three say* the sentence that the paragraph is in no passage gives way to its
quotation, and the new paragraph under *On what the division is* sets the fifth sentence beside
B 311 and B 307 and the footnote beside B 311's division of concepts, names the three places
of »intelligibel« in the map's Kant passages as unrelated by the map, and says why neither a
premise nor an attack is set against § 21 and GD Vernunft 2 and 6. The gaps list now says the
paragraph is held, counts its six sentences, two in two claims, names the four and the
footnote's first sentence as unclaimed with the reason, says that the chapter's text from
3:211.15 to its end is in the map, and names, for the first time, a stretch no node had named:
3:210.12–24, on B 308, from the sentence beginning *Wo diese Zeiteinheit* to the words
*Noumenon in positiver Bedeutung*, between the map's B 307–308 (3:209.32–210.12) and B 308–309
(3:210.24–34), in no passage; `memory.md` had carried "the rest of B 307–315 is held", which was
false of those twelve lines since run 2 and is corrected. The closing paragraph is dated; the
status note is unchanged, its clause on cells added since 2026-10-10 covering today's. Nine
existing nodes carry a dated sentence: the three passages and two claims of runs 24 and 25 whose
sentences said the paragraph was in no passage; the A 541 passage and claim, whose list of the
map's other Kant sentences with »Noumenon« gains the paragraph's fifth and sixth; and B 307's
negative-sense claim, which now names the sixth sentence as reporting an answer the map reads
as its own, with no link. README counts 306 nodes (3 thinkers, 5 works, 89 passages, 189
claims, 14 arguments, 4 readings, 2 comparisons), as `--check` does. No argument was
formalized.

**The adversarial pass, and what it found.** A second agent was given the five new nodes, the
diff, the korpora pages AA III 210–213, the wikisource transcriptions of A 255–258 and the scan
of A 256, the rendered build, the conventions and the distilled rules, with the brief to re-extract
both originals and verify every AA line, the page ends and catchwords, the 1781 collation comma
by comma, the footnote's absence from the 1781 pages and its line breaks, every translation
qualifier and resolution, every statement's mode, every link refused by grep over the other
statements, the placements by one criterion against every Kant claim on the page and the
unclaimed sentences, every »...« on the page and in the bodies, the accretion sweep, front matter
against prose and the rendered pages. It confirmed both originals character for character, all
six line ranges, the page ends, every collation variant and that none was missed, the orthography
list, the footnote's absence from A 256 on the scan and from A 257 on the transcription, the five
printed lines, the quote blocks, the front matter, the build and the rendering, and returned 17
findings, 1 serious, 8 moderate, 8 minor, and eight judgements; all 17 accepted and changed before
commit, and one judgement, on the reason for leaving the paragraph's first four sentences
unclaimed, taken against the run's first decision.
- *Serious, 1.* The fifth sentence's claim refused links by grepping »denkbar« and »intelligibel«
  and not the proposition: B 310's two claims in the row on what can be said, the concept of a
  thing »welches gar nicht als Gegenstand der Sinne, sondern als ein Ding an sich selbst
  (lediglich durch einen reinen Verstand) gedacht werden soll« and the possibility of such
  noumena not to be seen, describe the noumenon in the sentence's own terms, thought by the pure
  understanding alone and not as an object of the senses, and went unnamed; both now named on
  the node and, in one clause, on the page, the identification the map's, with no link.
- *Moderate, 8.* B 314's »immer unbekannt bleiben« placed "two paragraphs on" where it stands in
  the paragraph after: corrected. The stretch named for the first time as in no passage, on
  B 308, given as 3:210.12–23 where the words that close it stand on 3:210.24, the line on which
  B 308–309 begins: 3:210.12–24 on both pages, both end lines shared with held passages. The
  transcription's faces counted as four where it marks three, the sixth sentence's »Noumenon«
  unmarked, and the scan claimed for a term on A 257, a page the run had read on the transcription
  only: corrected. »auch noch einigen Gebrauch« rendered "still have any use" with the »auch«
  absorbed and unnamed: named on passage and claim. The sixth sentence's claim named A 236 / B 295
  and not its row-mate Prolegomena § 57, nor the Prolegomena's sentences on the restricted use of
  reason: each named with why no link. The footnote's claim did not name B 307, which names one
  thing by the kind of intuition of which it is or is not an object, the note's own criterion, nor
  B xxviii's two kinds of representation: named, the identification the map's, no link. The
  paragraph's first four sentences were left unclaimed by a term test, "name no noumenon in the
  chapter's sense", where the second names »die Sinnenwelt« and »die Verstandeswelt«, the words
  that set B 311 in the row on the division, and the first and fourth pass Kant's outright
  verdict on that division; the reviewer's preferred reason taken, that the division the four
  report and judge is the moderns' division of »den Inbegriff der Erscheinungen« into a world
  intuited and a world thought under laws, a division within appearances and not the row's, so
  that what they say that division is not is said of another division, written on the passage and
  in the gaps list as the map's reading, open to dispute. "The thing »an sich«" claimed for a
  sentence that says »er an sich« of the object: "the object »an sich«".
- *Minor, 8, all changed:* "answers in the negative" for a sentence that reports the question as
  already answered; »die Neueren« and »die Alten« quoted in the nominative where the text has the
  genitive; "one's own convenience" for »zu seiner Gemächlichkeit«, now "one's convenience"; the
  colon of »Es ist also die Frage: ob« dropped unnamed, now named; »Nach demselben« unresolved, now
  resolved as the first sentence's »Gebrauch«; the title's "as noumenon" a resolution of »in diesem
  Sinne« the body did not name, now named, and the title's "not to the senses" restored to "not at
  all"; »dieses Ausdrucks«, singular, read as the pair »mundi sensibilis und intelligibilis« with
  the nearer alternative, *mundus intelligibilis* alone, unnamed, now named on the passage, and the
  claim's "the two Latin terms" made "the expression the marked sentence names"; A 258's passage
  still gave »beantwortet haben« without guillemets, written so when the words were in no passage,
  now quoted and linked, the one accretion the run's sweep missed.
- *Judgements, eight:* the negative answer read as Kant's own (the attack that the sentence asserts
  only a question fails, »beantwortet haben« being an indicative factive report), the fifth
  sentence as one claim with three parts (accepted with the reservation that a third of the
  statement is a question the sixth sentence's claim carries again; the run keeps the whole
  sentence, as B 311's »obgleich« clause was kept, and the body says which part sets the row), the
  footnote's prescription in the row on the division, no `depends_on` to B 308–309, "word-mongering",
  the passive for »pflegt« and the unverified B pages, all in the direction taken; the reason for
  the four unclaimed sentences against it, changed as above.
The pass cost about 175,000 tokens of the agent's own and took ten minutes. New kinds today: a
no-link verdict grepped by term and not by proposition, which missed the thinker's own nearest
description of the same object; a gap's end line given one short of the words that close it; a
transcription's faces counted without opening the page; the scan read for one page and claimed
for two. All in `memory.md`.

**What else was wrong and why.**
- **This entry is in the run's second commit.** The first, `d439b52`, pushed the nodes, the page,

  rule, never put the commit in the same call as a script that may assert, is in `memory.md`.

  words; a third commit, the run's last, brings it to 7,990 and adds this sentence.

- `memory.md` carried "the rest of B 307–315 is held" since 2026-10-30; B 308's 3:210.12–24 was in

every new cell claim, and, on a Kant span, the A pages checked on wikisource before writing and,
where a catchword is a word's tail or a note is in doubt, on the scan.

**Effort.** About 285,000 of the 300,000-token ceiling in this session by the session's own count, plus
about 175,000 in the adversarial agent: a third on reading the governing files, the memory, the
last three entries and the page; a quarter on the sources, the scan, the five nodes and the page;
the rest on the wait, the 17 fixes, the memory compression, which took six passes, the premature
commit and the records. Nothing spent.

(3:213.22–36, four sentences) and
`p-krv-a258-260-b314-315-concept-of-purely-intelligible-objects-empty-of-all-principles-of-its-application`

was left in no passage. The 1781 pages A 256–260 were read on wikisource before anything was
written: the first paragraph taken stands whole on A 258, which opens with it (A 257 ends
»beantwortet haben«, catchword »Wenn«); the second begins on A 258, which ends at »oder nur die
Einheit«, runs over A 259, which ends at »einzuschränken,« with the catchword »ohne«, and
closes on A 260, where the Anhang on the amphiboly begins; the B pages are the concordance's
and unverified. The 1781 text differs in one word, »die empirische Grundsätze« for »die
empirischen Grundsätze«, and in punctuation, six added commas in the first paragraph's first
sentence and, in the second paragraph, a comma, a colon and a semicolon turned comma, listed
sentence by sentence on the passages; the Akademie text, as korpora renders it, sets nothing in
either paragraph in another face, and the words wikisource bolds (»wie sie erscheinen,«, »wie
sie sind,«, »Verstand«, »Sinnlichkeit«, »nur in Verbindung«) are named.

*The four claims, all `draft`, each with its quote.* From the first paragraph: its first
sentence, that if we say »die Sinne stellen uns die Gegenstände vor, wie sie erscheinen, der
Verstand aber, wie sie sind«, the latter »ist ... nicht in transscendentaler, sondern bloß
empirischer Bedeutung zu nehmen«, a conditional on a saying, kept conditional, its opening
»denn also« dropped and named; its second, the ground by »Denn« (3:213.29), that what objects
may be »außer der Beziehung auf mögliche Erfahrung ... mithin als Gegenstände des reinen
Verstandes« »wird uns immer unbekannt bleiben, so gar, daß es auch unbekannt bleibt, ob eine
solche transscendentale (außerordentliche) Erkenntniß überall möglich sei«, the »dieses«
resolved by name, the first claim set to `depends_on` this one by the connective, the one link
of the day; its third and fourth together, »Verstand und Sinnlichkeit können bei uns nur in
Verbindung Gegenstände bestimmen«, and apart they leave »Vorstellungen, die wir auf keinen
bestimmten Gegenstand beziehen können«. From the second paragraph its last sentence, »So ist
denn der Begriff reiner, bloß intelligibeler Gegenstände gänzlich leer von allen Grundsätzen
ihrer Anwendung«, the problematic thought of them serving »nur wie ein leerer Raum, die
empirischen Grundsätze einzuschränken«, the »So ... denn« dropped and said to draw from the
six sentences before it, a challenge, two questions and a verdict in the passage and in no
claim, so that no `depends_on` is set. Terms: »Bedeutung« "sense" as at B 308–309, »Sinne
überhaupt« "the senses in general", »überall möglich« "possible at all" as at B 311, »so gar,
daß« "so much so that", »zum wenigsten« "at least", »bei uns« "in our case", »ersinnen«
"devise", »aufweisen« "exhibit" kept apart from »nachweisen« "show", »Behauptung« "assertion",
»Satz« "proposition" and »Grundsatz« "principle" kept apart; »der letzteren« resolved as the
empirical principles. No claim names the thing in itself by Kant's term: the first two say

nodes say the map supplies no term, the identification of the fourth's objects with the

*The links.* One `depends_on`, above, with its converse, and, after the review, one shared

**The adversarial pass, and what it found.** A second agent was given the six new nodes, the

  four words apart (the pre-review grep had passed it, the pieces being substrings): the article
  dropped. Text in no passage quoted in guillemets twice (A 257's close, 3:213.16's »bloß
  intelligibel«): referred to without them. »ihrer Anwendung«, a genitive plural, rendered "its
  application" with no note: the resolution named on passage and claim, with the candidates

  phrase B 314 has as »außer der Beziehung auf ... Sinne überhaupt«; the reviewer's preferred fix
  taken, the claim entered in a second entry under the premise, the bridge named as Kant's own
  apposition and the map's step, open to dispute, on the node, the Schopenhauer node and the
  page. "A conditional whose antecedent Kant does not assert" characterized Kant without a
  passage; the no-link verdict now rests on the consequent's form, a prescription, »ist ... zu
  nehmen«, with "in no passage in the map asserted as Kant's own", on node and page. The B 314
  claim said the map supplies no term where the B 315 claim gave the identification with the
  chapter's noumena as the map's reading: now both do. The criterion that set the two was not
  applied to the older Kant claims on the page, and Prolegomena § 57's »würde es Ungereimtheit
  sein ... nur auf das mindeste Erkenntniß Anspruch machten, es ... wie es an sich selbst ist, zu
  bestimmen«, in the map since 2026-10-07 and in no row of either comparison, passes it: set in
  the row on what can be said, with a quote block added, a dated sentence on the node and the
  quotation under *What the three say*. The row-4 note dropped »zum wenigsten als eine solche,
  die unter unseren gewöhnlichen Kategorien steht«: restored. "The nearest sentences are these"

## 2026-10-30 — run 24: Kant's chapter on phenomena and noumena at A 254–256 / B 310–312, the concept of a noumenon not contradictory, necessary, its possibility not to be seen, a limiting concept of negative use, the division into phenomena and noumena not admissible in a positive sense, and the understanding thinking things in themselves only under the name of an unknown something

**Step 4, the nodes: 8 new (2 passages, 6 claims), 5 revised, the first page touched in two rows and five places of its prose, one attack link set.**
The plan's next content item was a gap the first page lists that adds a cell: the chapter on
phenomena and noumena beyond its opening and B 307–309, named in that page's list of what the
map does not yet contain since 2026-10-10. The two paragraphs that follow the map's B 308–309

on the division of the world into a world of sense and a world of understanding, were taken
from korpora.org's Akademie text (AA III 211–212) as two passages,
`p-krv-a254-255-b310-311-concept-of-a-noumenon-a-limiting-concept` (3:211.15–212.02, six
sentences) and

(3:212.03–27, six sentences), and the 1781 pages A 254, 255 and 256 were read on wikisource
before anything was written: A 254 ends at »(denn das übrige,« and A 255 at »daß unsere
Categorien,«, so the third sentence of the first paragraph and the second of the second each
straddle a page break; the B pages are the concordance's and unverified. The 1781 text differs
in wording at four places in the second paragraph and two in the first, the one of substance
being that the first edition refuses the division »gar nicht« where the second refuses it »in
positiver Bedeutung gar nicht«; the map holds the Akademie text, the second edition's, and
names the first's reading on the passage, the claim and the page. korpora's »Sinnen= und
Verstandeswelt« is written with the ordinary hyphen and the Latin terms' tag spaces are
normalized, the two normalizations named.

*The six claims, all `draft`, each with its quote.* From the first paragraph, sentences 2 to 6:
the concept of a noumenon, »eines Dinges, welches gar nicht als Gegenstand der Sinne, sondern
als ein Ding an sich selbst ... gedacht werden soll, ist gar nicht widersprechend«; the concept
»nothwendig, um die sinnliche Anschauung nicht bis über die Dinge an sich selbst auszudehnen«;
»die Möglichkeit solcher Noumenorum gar nicht einzusehen« and the range outside the sphere of
appearances »(für uns) leer«; the concept »bloß ein Grenzbegriff ... und also nur von negativem
Gebrauche«, yet »nicht willkürlich erdichtet«, the fifth and sixth sentences one claim, drawn
from the fourth by »also« (3:211.35), the one `depends_on` set, said to be the map's reading of
what the connective draws from. From the second paragraph, its first sentence, the division
»in Phaenomena und Noumena und der Welt in eine Sinnen- und Verstandeswelt kann daher in
positiver Bedeutung gar nicht zugelassen werden«, and its fifth and sixth, the understanding's
»negative Erweiterung«, calling things in themselves »(nicht als Erscheinungen betrachtet)
Noumena« and thinking them »nur unter dem Namen eines unbekannten Etwas«, the name
Prolegomena § 32 gives the thing in itself, the identification the map's. Terms: »Grenzbegriff«
"limiting concept" and »Begrenzung« "limitation" (*Grenze* "limit" throughout), »einschränken«
"restrict", »in Schranken setzend« "sets bounds to", »Anmaßung« "pretension" as at B xxx,
»einsehen« "see" as at B 307, »erdichtet« "invented", »zulassen« "admit", »ausgeben für« "pass
off as", Kant's »überall« "at all"; the two »jene« of the first paragraph resolved by name, the
second as sensible intuition after the paragraph before's use of the same verb, the candidates
named. In a passage and in no claim: the definition of a problematic concept, the question on
what the categories would mean apart from the senses, and the third and fourth sentences of the
second paragraph, which fall under the row's criterion and are left by this run's choice,
named on the passage and in the gaps list.

*The links.* One attack: JGB 16's claim that »"Ding an sich"« includes a contradictio in
adjecto, set since 2026-10-09 against B xxvi's sentence that things in themselves must at
least be thinkable, is set today also against the claim that the concept of a thing thought as
a thing in itself is »gar nicht widersprechend«, where the attacker's words meet the target's
without a step; the JGB 16 node says so, and as before no sentence of the aphorism names Kant.
No shared premise: the nearest, named on the nodes with the reason, are the premise on the laws
of experience (ZA II 516, laws and not intuition), § 1's »erträumtes Unding« (an object in
itself, not a concept), § 22's »unbekannte Größe ... Etwas« (the identification of will and
noumenon and of »erkannt« and »erkennen« refused, as under *On immediacy*), GD Vernunft 6's
second proposition (showing a reality, not seeing a possibility) and fourth (a division into a
true and an apparent world, not into a world of sense and a world of understanding, neither
premise nor attack), Fabel's stage 4 (narrated) and stage 5 (an idea refuted, not a concept
invented), and JGB 21's invented causes.

*The page.* `x-thing-in-itself`: five claims appended to the Kant cell of the row on what can
be said of the thing in itself and the division sentence to the Kant cell of the row on the

the end); the two passages in `passages:`; the closing paragraph dated. The status note is

**The adversarial pass, and what it found.** A second agent was given the eight new nodes, the

It confirmed both originals verbatim, every line and ordinal, the page breaks and catchwords,

- *Serious, 3.* The two collation notes missed nine variants of the 1781 scan: two commas in

## 2026-10-28 — run 22: the first page's row on the division filled from Kant's resolution of the third antinomy and preface, appearances as mere representations that must have grounds that are not appearances, and the eleventh shared premise, an appearance is a representation and nothing more

Twenty-second scheduled run, on `main`, on the configured model `claude-fable-5-1` (the same the
last entry records; no handover). Read all governing files first. The run fired the day after

over from run 21 that needed no new source: the comparison on the thing in itself, `checked` with the
owner's status note, had a row on the division of appearance from thing in itself and what follows from
refusing it, in which Kant's cell held B xxvi's absurdity and Prolegomena § 57's greater absurdity, while
the map held since 2026-10-27, in a passage and in no claim, the one sentence of the first Critique in
the map in which Kant says in his own voice what appearances are, A 537's »Wenn dagegen Erscheinungen für
nichts mehr gelten, als sie in der That sind, nämlich nicht für Dinge an sich, sondern bloße Vorstellungen,
die nach empirischen Gesetzen zusammenhängen, so müssen sie selbst noch Gründe haben, die nicht
Erscheinungen sind« (AA 3:365.21–25, A 536–537 / B 564–565), and, as a claim set on the will page only,
A 536's »Denn sind Erscheinungen Dinge an sich selbst, so ist Freiheit nicht zu retten« (3:365.17–18),
which the node and both gaps lists said belonged also in that row. The passage was re-read against
korpora's Akademie text, fetched again for the line numbers; no new span was taken.

*The claim.* `c-kant-appearances-mere-representations-must-have-grounds-that-are-not-appearances`, `draft`,
with its quote: the antecedent carries Kant's own assertion of what appearances are, »als sie in der That
sind«, and the statement sets that first and asserted; the consequent, the grounds that are not
appearances, stands under the »wenn« and the statement keeps it there; »dagegen« dropped and named;
»für ... gelten« "count for", »zusammenhängen« "cohere", »noch« "still", »selbst« "themselves". The
sentence says »Gründe« and the next calls such a ground »eine solche intelligibele Ursache«; it does not
say »Ding an sich« of the grounds, and whether they are the thing in itself of B xxvi or the
»transscendentaler Gegenstand« of A 538 no passage in the map says: three Kant sentences give what
appearances need three names, no connective joins the three places, and the map sets no link among them,
does not set the claim in the row on whether there is a thing in itself to a given appearance, and does
not enter it under that shared premise, said on the node and in that premise's paragraph.

*The shared premise, the eleventh on the page.* »An appearance is a representation and nothing more«:
Kant's »bloße Vorstellungen«, asserted by »in der That sind«, and Schopenhauer's § 21 »Erscheinung heißt
Vorstellung, und weiter nichts« (ZA I 153–154), the sentence § 21 sets before »Ding an sich aber ist
allein der Wille«. Both statements assert it, in the one word both texts use; no passage in the map says
how Schopenhauer's »Vorstellung« stands to Kant's, and the paragraph says that the sharing rests on the two
statements, as the convention asks, not on a history of the term, open to dispute. Named as nearest and
not entered: A 538's »der sie als bloße Vorstellungen bestimmt«, where the words sit inside a relative
clause on what the transcendental object does, and § 1's »Vorstellung«, whose subject is the world.
`shares_premise_with` set on both claims; the Schopenhauer node's body carries the entry and the
sentence that its converse, that all representation is appearance, is § 21's alone.

*The cell.* The Kant cell of the row on the division holds, after B xxvi and Prolegomena § 57, five
more by one criterion, what the sentence says appearances are and what follows, for freedom, from taking
them for things in themselves, the row's second question: B xxvii's two sentences on the distinction »gar
nicht gemacht«, until today set on the will page only (in the map since 2026-10-11); A 536's conditional;
the new claim; and A 538's »da« clause, in the map since 2026-10-19 and until today on no comparison,
that because appearances »an sich keine Dinge sind« a transcendental object »zum Grunde liegen muß, der
sie als bloße Vorstellungen bestimmt«. The note is restructured by mode: what follows, each under a
supposition Kant does not assert, then what appearances are, asserted; it says that the preface's case
and the resolution's are one as the map reads the two, drawn by no connective from each other, so that no
link is set. Under *On what the division is* a passage sets Kant's answer to what appearances are beside
Schopenhauer's »heißt Vorstellung«, names the four places the Kant passages in the map say what refusing
the division costs, each under a supposition (B xxvi's »sonst«, § 57's »wenn«, B xxvii's »wäre gar nicht
gemacht«, A 536's »sind ... so«), sets § 24's conditional beside A 537's asserted antecedent, and says
that no Nietzsche statement in the map denies in its words that appearances are representations that must
have grounds, GD's »absolut unnachweisbar« denying showing and not grounds, so no attack link. The
paragraph on the premise that there is a thing in itself to a given appearance names A 537's grounds
and A 538's transcendental object the two Kant sentences nearest to it and not entered, with the reason.
Three passages added to `passages:`; the gaps list now names the resolution's eight passages as set on
the will page and says which of their sentences this page holds and why the rest add no cell here; the

**The adversarial pass, and what it found.** A second agent was given the diff, the new node, the

the brief to re-extract and verify every AA line cited, to check every quotation against its passage,

- *Serious, 1: a sentence true until today.* The will page's paragraph on A 536 said that the sentence

- *Judgements, six, all in the direction taken,* recorded: reading »als sie in der That sind« as

## 2026-10-27 — run 21: Kant's A 532–537, freedom in the cosmological and the practical sense, the human power of choice as arbitrium liberum, and the effect regardable as free and as necessary

Twenty-first scheduled run, on `main`, on the configured model `claude-fable-5-1` (the same the
last entry records; no handover). Read all governing files first. The run fired the day after
run 20; `origin/main` stood at run 20's commit when it began, and nothing arrived in between.

**Step 1.** No ask changed status. Asks 4 (deferred), 9 and 10 stay open; none blocks the
plan.

**Step 2.** `INBOX.md` carries the owner notes of 2026-10-11 and 2026-10-19, each handled in
the session that received it; nothing new. No dispute, no attestation, no form filing (the
workflow made no commit).

**Step 3.** No open motion.

**Step 4, the nodes: 14 new, 11 revised, the will page touched in one row and five places of its prose.**
The plan's next content item was a gap the pages list that adds a cell. The will page's gaps list
named, first among Kant's, the resolution of the third antinomy before A 538–541, »A 532–537 / B
560–565 before it«, and since run 20 the same pages stand on the page a second time as what § 55's
footnote cites; the row on whether the will is free, and free of what, had Kant's cell set from
B xxvii–xxix, conditional throughout, and from A 538–541, under »würde« throughout, and the paragraph
under *On freedom* said that Schopenhauer's negative concept of freedom had no counterpart in a Kant
passage the map held. A 532–537 is where the first Critique says outright what freedom is, in two
senses, and it was taken in three spans from korpora.org's Akademie text (AA III 362–366), the A
pages checked against the page-by-page transcription of the 1781 scan on de.wikisource.org before
anything was written.

*The three passages.* `p-krv-a532-533-two-kinds-of-causality-freedom-in-the-cosmological-sense`, the
first six sentences of section III, from »Man kann sich nur zweierlei Causalität« to »zur Handlung zu
bestimmen« (AA 3:362.29–363.24; A 532–533 / B 560–561); `p-krv-a533-534-freedom-in-the-practical-sense`,
the second and third paragraphs whole, six sentences from »Es ist überaus merkwürdig« to »ganz von
selbst anzufangen« (3:363.25–364.15; A 533–534 / B 561–562, the definition of practical freedom
straddling the 1781 page break at »Die«); and
`p-krv-a536-537-if-appearances-are-things-in-themselves-freedom-not-to-be-saved`, seven sentences of
the fifth paragraph from »Und hier zeigt die zwar gemeine« to »in der Anwendung aufklären wird«
(3:365.14–35; A 536–537 / B 564–565). Named on the passages: korpora's »Bedarf« with a capital where
the 1781 scan reads »bedarf«, kept as transcribed; the transcription's spaces inside the tag that sets
the Latin terms, »( arbitrium brutum )«, normalized to the Akademie-Ausgabe's »(arbitrium brutum)«,
the one normalization made; the 1781 edition's letterspacing of »Freiheit im practischen Verstande«,
not reproduced; the B page breaks inside B 560–565 unverified, the AA line the locator. Terms new to
the map: »Willkür« "power of choice", never "will", since the map holds Kant's »Wille« at B xxvii and
no passage says how the two words relate; »Nöthigung« "necessitation", »Antriebe« "impulses",
»Bewegursachen« "motive causes", »beiwohnt« "dwells in", »Aufhebung« "annulment" (as »aufheben« is
"annul" at B xxx), »vertilgen« "destroy", »retten« "save", »Erfolg« "result", »Inbegriff« "sum",
»Spontaneität« "spontaneity", »anheben« "begin", »vorangeschickt« "sent ahead".

*The eleven claims, all `draft`, each with its quote, all set in the Kant cell of the row on freedom
after the A 538–541 claims, for the words »Freiheit« and »Willkür« (two were on no row until the review; below).* (1) »Man kann sich
nur zweierlei Causalität in Ansehung dessen, was geschieht, denken, entweder nach der Natur, oder aus
Freiheit«, the frame; (4) Kant's own definition,
»verstehe ich unter«, carried as "as Kant understands the term": freedom in the cosmological sense is
»das Vermögen, einen Zustand von selbst anzufangen, deren Causalität also nicht nach dem Naturgesetze
wiederum unter einer anderen Ursache steht, welche sie der Zeit nach bestimmte«, »deren« read as the
faculty's causality and the reading open to dispute; (5) freedom in that sense »eine reine
transscendentale Idee« whose object »in keiner Erfahrung bestimmt gegeben werden kann«, the closing
»wodurch denn« clause dropped and named; (6) reason »schafft sich ... die Idee von einer Spontaneität,
die von selbst anheben könne zu handeln, ohne daß eine andere Ursache vorangeschickt werden dürfe«,
the idea asserted as reason's and its content under »könne«; (7) »Es ist überaus merkwürdig, daß« the
practical concept of freedom »sich ... gründe« on the transcendental idea, the frame kept, »jene« read
as the idea and »dieser« as practical freedom; (8) »Die Freiheit im praktischen Verstande ist die
Unabhängigkeit der Willkür von der Nöthigung durch Antriebe der Sinnlichkeit«; (10) »Die menschliche
Willkür ist zwar ein arbitrium sensitivum, aber nicht brutum, sondern liberum, weil Sinnlichkeit ihre
Handlung nicht nothwendig macht, sondern dem Menschen ein Vermögen beiwohnt, sich unabhängig von der
Nöthigung durch sinnliche Antriebe von selbst zu bestimmen«, the one sentence in the map in which Kant
asserts a human faculty free; (11) under »wenn alle Causalität in der Sinnenwelt bloß Natur wäre«,
the annulment of transcendental freedom »würde ... zugleich alle praktische Freiheit vertilgen«, »Man
sieht leicht, daß« dropped and named, drawn by »Denn« from (12) practical freedom »setzt voraus, daß
... nicht in unserer Willkür eine Causalität liege ... mithin eine Reihe von Begebenheiten ganz von
selbst anzufangen«, the one link set today, `depends_on` with the connective quoted; (14) »Denn sind
Erscheinungen Dinge an sich selbst, so ist Freiheit nicht zu retten«, which belongs also on the
comparison on the thing in itself, in the row on the division, not touched today, said on the node; and (19) »Die Wirkung kann also in Ansehung ihrer intelligibelen
Ursache als frei und doch zugleich in Ansehung der Erscheinungen als Erfolg aus denselben nach der
Nothwendigkeit der Natur angesehen werden«, what can be regarded, the modal kept, the apposition on the
distinction's subtlety dropped and named. No shared premise and no attack, argued on each node: Kant's
definitions are neither one proposition with Schopenhauer's negative concept nor denials of it; A 534's
»liberum« of the human »Willkür« beside § 55's person who »ist nie frei« is the nearest denial in the
map, and whether the person is the bearer of the power of choice and »nie frei« denies »liberum«, two
senses of free, no passage says, the identification the map's; A 537's »kann ... angesehen werden«
beside § 55's »Jedes Ding ist als Erscheinung ... nothwendig: dasselbe ist an sich Wille« is the nearest
unset premise, a modal asserting what can be regarded and not what is; A 536's conditional beside § 55's
»folgt schon daraus« makes freedom turn on the distinction from opposite sides; the spontaneity that
begins to act without a prior cause beside JGB 21's causa sui, the identification the map's, the denial
needing two Nietzsche sentences joined and hitting a content Kant does not assert; and GD 7, GD 8 and
JGB 21's mythology sentence deny none of the eleven in their words. Each absence is open to dispute,
said on the nodes.

*The page.* `x-the-will`: the eleven claims appended to the Kant cell of the row on freedom and the
row's note extended, saying that the cell is set for »Freiheit« and »Willkür« and that the map does not
identify the power of choice with the will; the sentence under *On freedom* that had the resolution of
the antinomy »under »würde« throughout« narrowed to its section A 538–541; the sentence counting »the
five Kant passages listed on this page« extended; the sentence that Schopenhauer's negative concept
»has no counterpart in a Kant passage the map holds«, true since 2026-10-26 and false today, dated and
answered; a paragraph added under *On freedom* on A 532–537, listing free-of-what in Kant's four
sentences and Schopenhauer's two, and what is and is not parallel to § 55 and to JGB 21; a clause added
to the JGB 21 paragraph; a passage added under *Kant and Schopenhauer on the law and the thing in
itself* naming A 537 the nearest unset premise on the page; the three passages in `passages:`; the
gaps list, which now names the nineteen sentences of the three spans, eleven in claims, which eight are
in no claim and which paragraphs of section III are in no passage, and keeps A 542 ff.; the closing
paragraph. Seven existing nodes received a dated sentence on today's Kant sentences, by the rule of run
20 that the other thinkers' claim bodies carry absences the page sweep cannot see: § 55's negative
concept of freedom (its sentence that no Kant statement in the map said what freedom is, dated and
answered), the person never free (A 534 the nearest denial), every thing necessary as appearance (A 537
the nearest unset premise), the will as such free (A 536 beside it), JGB 21's causa sui desire and its
self-contradiction (A 533's spontaneity the nearest), and the unfree will as mythology (A 534). README
counts 286 nodes (3 thinkers, 5 works, 83 passages, 175 claims, 14 arguments, 4 readings, 2
comparisons); the colophon agrees. No argument was formalized. The comparison on the thing in itself
is untouched, and today's fourth sentence of A 536–537, that appearances are in fact mere
representations that must have grounds that are not appearances, is held in the passage and in no
claim for a run that touches that page, said on the passage, on the gaps list and in `memory.md`.

**The adversarial pass, and what it found.** A second agent was given the fourteen new nodes, the
diff of the page and of the seven edited nodes, the korpora pages and the wikisource transcriptions
of A 532–538 with the extraction script, the conventions and the distilled rules, with the brief to
re-extract and collate the originals by script, check the A pages and every page-break claim, count
the sentences and the ordinals, check every quote and translation, every statement's qualifiers and
mode, every asserted absence and every "the nearest" against every statement in the map, whether any
link is due, the fit and reason of each cell, the page prose for generalizations and accretion, the
format and the rendered pages. It confirmed the three originals verbatim, the A pages and page breaks,
the paragraph numbering, the counts 6 + 6 + 7 and every ordinal and AA line, every quote block, the
statements' modes and qualifiers, the uniqueness claims against all 71 Kant statements, the accretion
sweep of the row notes, both pages, the thinker and work nodes and the README, the one link and its
converse, every absence under the convention, the format and the build, and returned 22 findings, 4
serious, 7 moderate, 11 minor, and nine judgements; all 22 accepted and changed before commit, and
five judgements taken.
- *Serious, 4: one translation, three accretions.* The English of A 533's first sentence, "the former
  constitutes in the latter", ran by the English order and so reversed the map's own reading of »jene
  ... in dieser« (the idea in practical freedom): the passage, the quote block and the bodies now
  resolve the pronouns by name, "this idea constitutes in practical freedom", and say why "the former"
  would not do; the rule is in `memory.md`. Three sentences true until today and false after it, none
  of which the run's own sweep had found, since the sweep grepped for "no Kant" and "no passage in the
  map" and not for page ranges: § 55's claim that Kant first showed the coexistence said that within
  A 532–558 the map holds A 538–541; its passage said the same of B 560–586; and A 538's own passage
  said that the text preceding its heading, 3:365.21–366.04, is not in the map. Each now names today's
  spans, and the rule in `memory.md` says to grep for "not in the map" and "the map holds" with the
  pages added and for the pages other thinkers' nodes cite.
- *Moderate, 7:* "the nearest" Kant sentence to GD 8's »intelligible Freiheit« named A 539's
  character in three places (the GD 8 node, the row note, *On freedom*) where A 537's »in Ansehung
  ihrer intelligibelen Ursache als frei« is the one Kant sentence in the map joining the adjective and
  »frei« in a clause not under »würde«, now named in all three; "A 536–537" on a sentence wholly on
  A 536; the new paragraph's opening claimed for the first Critique what only the map's holdings
  warrant and ran a circular clause (A 541 inside A 538–541), now "the first place among the Kant
  passages the map holds" and the two older texts' modes named; the row note's "asserted outright"
  governed a list holding the subjunctive »gründe« and the spontaneity's »könne«, now restructured by
  mode; the collation note listed orthography only, where the 1781 scan has two wording variants
  inside the spans, »in einen Inbegriff« and »und so durch andere Erscheinungen«, and three
  punctuation differences, now listed on all three passages; "letterspaced" for a phrase wikisource
  marks bold as it marks a dozen others, now "emphasized, as it marks a dozen other words"; and K1's
  absence sentence did not name JGB 36's »als die einzige«, the one Nietzsche statement on one kind of
  causality, now named with why no link is due.
- *Minor, 11, all changed:* »deren« could bind only to »Freiheit« (»Zustand« would take »dessen«);
  »selbst« moved from »selbst wiederum« to »auch entstanden« in a translation; »zwar« dropped from
  »die zwar gemeine, aber betrügliche«; »von selbst« "of himself" at 3:364.03 unnamed against "of
  itself" elsewhere; »aber« of »Da aber« dropped unnamed; "the two claims before it assert" where one
  defines; a clause that identified what the next denied; "as the map reads the two" missing on an
  identification of A 537 with A 541; the fifth unclaimed sentence of the third span undescribed in the
  gaps list; "every sentence of the practical sense" where the first has neither word; and a doubled

- *Judgements taken, 5:* the two claims set on no row because they do not name the will, where the
  definitions, which name it no more, were set for the word »Freiheit«, are now set in the row by the
  same criterion, so the cell holds eleven and the first page's row on the division is named as the

  »bestimmte« called preterite, not subjunctive; "not conditional" of A 537 now "not under »wenn« or
  »würde«"; "the section before" now "the untitled opening of section III before the subsection".
  *Declined:* naming JGB 21's »die Freiheit« among the invented signs against the transcendental idea

## 2026-10-26 — run 20: Schopenhauer's § 55, the will as such free since it is the thing in itself, the person never free, and Kant's distinction of intelligible and empirical character retained

Twentieth scheduled run, on `main`, on the configured model `claude-fable-5-1` (the same the
last entry records; no handover). Read all governing files first. The run fired the day after
run 19; `origin/main` stood at run 19's commit when it began, and nothing arrived in between.
The container had a detached HEAD at that commit; `main` was checked out to it.

**Step 1.** No ask changed status. Asks 4 (deferred), 9 and 10 stay open; none blocks the
plan.

**Step 2.** `INBOX.md` carries the owner notes of 2026-10-11 and 2026-10-19, each handled in
the session that received it; nothing new. No dispute, no attestation, no form filing (the
workflow made no commit).

**Step 3.** No open motion.

**Step 4, the nodes: 10 new, 3 revised, the will page touched in one row.** The plan's next
content item was a gap the pages list that adds a cell. The will page's row on whether the will is
free, and free of what, had Schopenhauer's cell set for § 23's word »frei« alone, freedom from the
forms, and its note said outright that no passage in the map had Schopenhauer on the freedom of the
will in the sense of B xxvii; the gaps list named the fourth Book, »including § 55«. § 55 is where
the fourth Book answers that question in its own words, and it was taken, in three spans. zeno.org
answered (the Viertes Buch page), and its text, Hübscher's with the ZA pages, is the transcription;
Projekt Gutenberg-DE served Grisebach's chapter 57 the same day, and every span was collated word by
word against it by script before anything was written.

*The three passages.* `p-wwv1-s55-will-as-such-free-everything-as-appearance-necessary`, the
section's first eight sentences by Grisebach's full stops, from »Daß der Wille als solcher frei sei«
to »für alle Ewigkeit« (ZA I 360); `p-wwv1-s55-person-never-free`, two sentences later in the same
paragraph, from »Die Freiheit des Willens als Dinges an sich geht« to »mit der Gesetzmäßigkeit einer
Naturkraft sich darstellt« (ZA I 362); and `p-wwv1-s55-kant-intelligible-and-empirical-character`,
two sentences from »Die Nothwendigkeit des einzelnen Handelns hat Priestley« to »erfahrungsmäßig
darstellt« (ZA I 363). The collation found seven variants, all named on the passages: zeno's
»Beides«, »Ihre« after »der Natur,« and »Räume« in »dem Räume nach« (the last two read as the
transcription's slips, kept as transcribed), its consistent »d.h.«, a comma after »geredet worden«
where Grisebach prints the full stop that ends the seventh sentence, and Hübscher's »absehn« and
»Zusammenbestehn«; and one thing new: after »nachgewiesen« the ZA text carries the mark of
footnote 80, the reference to »Kritik der reinen Vernunft«, erste Auflage, S. 532–558; fünfte
Auflage, S. 560–586, and to the second Critique, and Projekt Gutenberg-DE prints the note's words
inside the sentence, without brackets, so that a span stored from Gutenberg alone would have carried
them as Schopenhauer's sentence. The mark is omitted as every footnote mark is, the note's text is
given on the passage, and the rule is in `memory.md`. Grisebach letterspaces »frei« twice and »an
sich« once in the opening, as emphasis, not reproduced and said on the passage. Terms new to the map:
»Wechselbegriffe« "convertible concepts", »Einheitspunkt« "point of unity", »außerzeitlich« "outside
time" (as § 25's »außer aller Zeit«), »Willensakt« "act of will", »Korporisation« "corporization",
»Handlungsweise« "manner of acting", »dargethan« "set forth" so that »nachgewiesen« keeps "shown".

*The seven claims, all `draft`, each with its quote, all set in the Schopenhauer cell of the row on
freedom after the three older.* (1) That the will as such is free »folgt schon daraus, daß er, nach
unserer Ansicht, das Ding an sich, der Gehalt aller Erscheinung ist«, "on the view of Schopenhauer's
work" in the statement so that it reads alone; (5) the world in all its appearances is objectity of
the will, which, being thing in itself, is not subject to »dem Satz vom Grunde, der Form alles
Objekts«, »also nicht als Folge durch einen Grund bestimmt ist, also keine Nothwendigkeit kennt,
d.h. frei ist«, the answer to free of what, the opening »Andererseits nun aber« dropped and named;
(6) »Der Begriff der Freiheit ist also eigentlich ein negativer«, its content »bloß die Verneinung
der Nothwendigkeit«, drawn by »also« from (5), the one link set today, `depends_on` with the
connective quoted; (8) »Jedes Ding ist als Erscheinung, als Objekt, durchweg nothwendig: dasselbe
ist an sich Wille, und dieser ist völlig frei, für alle Ewigkeit«; the person, »das vernünftige
Thier mit individuellem Charakter«, »ist nie frei, obwohl sie die Erscheinung eines freien Willens
ist«, the sentence that answers for the will in its actions, B xxvii's sense, and answers no; Kant,
»dessen Verdienst hier besonders groß ist«, was the first to show the coexistence of the necessity
of individual action with the freedom of the will in itself, »indem er den Unterschied zwischen
intelligiblem und empirischem Charakter aufstellte, welchen ich ganz und gar beibehalte«, with
Schopenhauer's own definitions of the two, the Priestley half of the sentence dropped and named; and
the intelligible character of every man »als ein außerzeitlicher, daher untheilbarer und
unveränderlicher Willensakt zu betrachten sei«, the frame naming the »einleitenden Abhandlung«
dropped and named, »zu betrachten sei« kept. No shared premise and no attack, argued on each node:
Kant's B xxvii has the same will »gedacht« as free under »Wenn aber die Kritik nicht geirrt hat«,
and the antinomy's two characters stand under »würde«, where § 55 asserts; Kant's will belongs to
a thing in itself where Schopenhauer's thing is in itself will, the hinge both pages name; Kant's
asserted »denn« clause on time is not what the sentence on the »außerzeitlicher Willensakt« states;
JGB 21 denies an unfree will where § 55 denies the person's freedom and calls the will free; and
whether GD 8's »noch er selbst« denies that a man's intelligible character is his own act of will
no sentence says, the identification would be the map's, and each absence is open to dispute, said
on the nodes.

*The page.* `x-the-will`: the seven claims appended to the Schopenhauer cell of the row on freedom;
the row's sentence that no passage in the map had Schopenhauer on the freedom of the will in the
sense of B xxvii, true since 2026-10-15 and false today, replaced by the sentences on § 55, and
"the three statements" made "the first three"; a passage added to *Kant and Schopenhauer on the law
and the thing in itself* on the two nearest unset premises, § 55's »auch nicht dem Satz vom Grunde
... unterworfen« beside Kant's B xxvii clause and »Jedes Ding ist als Erscheinung ... nothwendig:
dasselbe ist an sich Wille« beside the very same will of B xxvii–xxviii; a paragraph added at the
end of *On freedom* saying what is parallel to Kant in Schopenhauer's own sentence (the distinction
retained, the pages his footnote names, inside which the map's A 538–541 lies) and what is not (an
act of will asserted against »würde« predicates of a subject of the world of sense; the concept of
freedom negative, with no counterpart in a Kant passage the map holds; the person never free beside
»so fern nicht frei«), and beside Nietzsche (JGB 21's mythology; GD 8's »"intelligible Freiheit"«
and § 55's intelligible character the two sentences in the map naming a doctrine of Kant's by that
adjective, not identified); the gaps list, which now names the five sentences of the three spans in
no claim and § 55's remainder; the three passages in `passages:`; the closing paragraph. The work
and thinker nodes say where the fourth Book's first passages come from. README counts 272 nodes
(3 thinkers, 5 works, 80 passages, 164 claims, 14 arguments, 4 readings, 2 comparisons); the colophon
agrees. No argument was formalized. The comparison on the thing in itself is untouched: § 55 adds no
cell to its rows.

**The adversarial pass, and what it found.** A second agent was given the ten new nodes, the diff
of the page, the work and thinker nodes and the README, both corpus files (zeno.org's Viertes Buch
and Gutenberg's chapter 57) with the extracted spans, the older Kant, Schopenhauer and Nietzsche
nodes the bodies cite or argue absences against, the conventions and the distilled rules, with the
brief to re-extract and collate the originals by script, check the ZA pages, count the sentences
and the ordinals, check every quote and translation, every statement's qualifiers and mode, every
asserted absence against every statement in the map, whether any link is due, the fit and reason of
each cell, the page prose for generalizations and accretion, the format and the rendered pages. It
confirmed the three originals verbatim, the pages 360, 362 and 363, the seven collation variants
exactly, the counts 8 + 2 + 2 and every ordinal, the gaps list as exact, every quote and the twelve
translations' content, the one link and its converse, every absence under the convention, the
format and the build, and returned 26 findings, 4 serious, 11 moderate, 11 minor, and nine
judgements; all 26 accepted and changed before commit, and one judgement taken.
- *Serious, 4, all accretion: sentences true until today and false after it, on nodes and lines
  the run did not touch.* JGB 21's mythology claim said "no passage in the map has Schopenhauer on
  the latter", a will free or unfree in its actions; JGB 36's will-to-power claim said "no
  Schopenhauer passage in the map carries the term" »intelligiblen Charakter«; the older part of
  *On freedom* said no passage in the map has Schopenhauer on the freedom of the will in Kant's
  sense; and "Schopenhauer's two sentences in this row" counted a cell that now holds ten. Each now
  names § 55's sentence and links it. The run's own accretion sweep had grepped the page for the
  row note's sentence and missed these: the rule in `memory.md` now says to grep every claim body
  of the other thinkers for "no ... passage in the map" naming the thinker whose text is added.
- *Moderate, 11:* »außer aller Zeit« cited as § 25's phrase, which is »außer der Zeit und dem
  Raum«; § 21 misquoted (»das Ding an sich ist der Wille allein« for »Ding an sich aber ist allein
  der Wille«); the gaps list said the Priestley half-sentence is in no passage, where it is in the
  third span and dropped from its claim's statement, and paraphrased »hält ... sich ... für frei«;
  "the five Kant passages set in this row" where the fifth, A 538's definition, is on no row;
  Kant's »ersteren« and »zweiten« reversed in the body's "first" and "second"; the one Nietzsche
  statement carrying »"intelligiblen Charakter"«, JGB 36, not named against the two claims on the
  intelligible character, now named on both and on the page; JGB 21's »"Nothwendigkeit"«, the
  nearest word in the map to »keine Nothwendigkeit kennt«, not named, now quoted with the reason no
  premise is set; a sentence that Kant's negative and positive concepts of freedom stand in the
  Grundlegung and the second Critique, about Kant and citing nothing (P1), now names the works as
  absent only; the identification of »obigen ... Fall« with the will's self-denial stated flat in
  three places, resting on sentences in no passage, now "as the map reads"; the row note's "the
  sentence that answers for the will in its actions, the sense of B xxvii" outran a statement whose
  subject is the person, now "the will's appearance in its actions ... answers no of the person";
  and the letterspacing sentence implied a variant, where zeno.org italicizes the same three words,
  now "both transcriptions mark ... the map reproduces neither".
- *Minor, 11, all changed:* »Kants meisterhafte Darstellung« quoted from no passage; the fifth
  edition's pages identified with B without saying so; »einleitende« for the text's »einleitenden«,
  and the essay named as fact; »man ist nothwendig« for the passage's »Man«; the ground against
  Kant given twice on one node, the first deleted and the antinomy's »Freiheit und Natur« carried
  into the second; the second »aber« turned "and" unnamed; four translation qualifiers unnamed
  (»nun«, an added "and", »Gehalt« and »Inhalt« both "content", »Folge« twice); »nachgewiesen«
  "shown" against § 25's "demonstrated", now named; »unmittelbar« dropped from a passage body's
  summary; "adds freedom" where § 23 already has »völlig frei«, now "adds »keine Nothwendigkeit

- *Judgement taken:* »Ihre gesammten Erscheinungen« "all its appearances" for "its appearances

  muß« "must be there to be found", "on the view of Schopenhauer's work", kept as the reviewer's own

The pass cost about 186,000 tokens of the agent's own and took thirteen minutes. New kinds today:
accretion on the other thinkers' nodes, which the sweep of the page cannot see; a footnote folded
into a sentence by a transcription; Kant's own ordinals reversed by the body's; and an emphasis
named as one edition's that both carry. All in `memory.md`.

## 2026-10-25 — run 19: JGB 19 held whole, »Freiheit des Willens« as the affect of superiority and as the word for a state of pleasure, all willing as commanding and obeying

Nineteenth scheduled run, on `main`, on the configured model `claude-fable-5-1` (the same the
last entry records; no handover). Read all governing files first. The run fired the day after
run 18; `origin/main` stood at run 18's commit when it began, and nothing arrived in between.
The container had a detached HEAD at that commit; `main` was checked out to it.

**Step 1.** No ask changed status. Asks 4 (deferred), 9 and 10 stay open; none blocks the
plan.

**Step 2.** `INBOX.md` carries the owner notes of 2026-10-11 and 2026-10-19, each handled in
the session that received it; nothing new. No dispute, no attestation, no form filing (the
workflow made no commit).

**Step 3.** No open motion.

**Step 4, the nodes: 6 new, 3 revised, the will page touched in three rows.** The plan's next
content item was a gap the pages list that adds a cell; the will page named Nietzsche's JGB 19
beyond its two spans, the one Nietzsche gap left on it, and it was taken: the aphorism is now held
whole in four spans. The Gutenberg text (eBook 7204, the transcription of every JGB passage in the
map) was fetched, the two remaining spans extracted by start and end substring, and the four spans
asserted by script to concatenate to the whole aphorism, less the dash the transcription prints
after its last full stop, before anything was written.

*The two passages.* `p-jgb-19-freedom-of-the-will-the-affect-of-superiority`, the seventh and
eighth of the aphorism's fourteen sentences (full stop, question mark and exclamation mark; a colon
joins halves), from »Das, was "Freiheit des Willens" genannt wird« to »dass es gehorcht«; and
`p-jgb-19-close-freedom-of-the-will-the-word-for-a-state-of-pleasure`, the eleventh to the
fourteenth, from »"Freiheit des Willens" - das ist das Wort« to »entsteht«. Named on the passages:
the transcription's one kind of quotation mark at both levels in »"ich bin frei, "er" muss
gehorchen"«, kept; the closing dash not reproduced, now also said on `w-nietzsche-jgb`. Terms new
to the map: »Überlegenheits-Affekt« "affect of superiority", »Lust-Zustand« "state of pleasure",
»Unterwillen« "under-wills", »Gesellschaftsbau« "social edifice", »Gemeinwesen« "commonwealth",
»Herrschafts-Verhältnisse« "relations of domination", »Gesichtskreis« "purview"; »Noth« rendered
"needful" so that »Nothwendigkeit« keeps "necessity"; »Wollen an sich schon« "willing as such
already", never "in itself"; »dass gehorcht werden wird« "that there will be obedience", the noun
supplied where English has no impersonal passive.

*The four claims, all `draft`, each with its quote.* (7) What is called »"Freiheit des Willens"«
is »wesentlich der Überlegenheits-Affekt in Hinsicht auf Den, der gehorchen muss«, »"ich bin frei,
"er" muss gehorchen"«, a consciousness that lies in every will, with the tension of attention, the
gaze, the valuation and the inner certainty that there will be obedience; (8) »Ein Mensch, der will
-, befiehlt einem Etwas in sich, das gehorcht oder von dem er glaubt, dass es gehorcht«; (11)
»"Freiheit des Willens"« is the word for the manifold »Lust-Zustand des Wollenden, der befiehlt und
sich zugleich mit dem Ausführenden als Eins setzt«, who shares in the triumph over resistances but
judges to himself that his will itself overcomes them, the reported subjunctive carried by the
frame; (14) »Bei allem Wollen handelt es sich schlechterdings um Befehlen und Gehorchen« on a
»Gesellschaftsbau vieler "Seelen"«, »weshalb« a philosopher should claim the right to bring willing
as such already within the purview of morality, understood as the doctrine of the relations of
domination under which »"Leben"« arises, the »weshalb« inside one sentence, no argument and no
`depends_on`, as with GD 7's »folglich«; »wie gesagt« dropped from the statement and named. Sentences
12 (the under-wills and under-souls, »unser Leib ist ja nur ein Gesellschaftsbau vieler Seelen«, to
which »wie gesagt« points) and 13 (»L'effet c'est moi«) are in the passage and in no claim. No link
on any of the four, argued on the nodes: no Kant or Schopenhauer statement in the map speaks of
commanding, obeying, superiority, an affect, domination or pleasure, or of a consciousness of
freedom (B xxviii has freedom thought); Kant's »frei« is a predicate of the will as belonging to a
thing in itself, under the critique's conditional, and the antinomy's independence from natural
necessity, which none of the four denies; Schopenhauer's § 18 identity is denied by the ninth and
tenth sentences, already the attack, not by the eleventh, which has the one who wills posit himself
as one with the one who carries it out, a positing and not a denial; § 23's »frei« is freedom from
the forms; JGB 21's same term in another aphorism, JGB 17 and 54 on the subject, neither naming
the other, no link; and each absence is open to dispute, said on the nodes.

*The page.* `x-the-will`: (8) and (14) added to the Nietzsche cell of the row on what the will is,
for what a man who wills does and what all willing is a matter of; (7) and (11) to the Nietzsche
cell of the row on freedom, after JGB 21's six and before GD's four, for the term »Freiheit des
Willens«, the one saying what is so called, the other what the word is the word for; (14) also to
the Nietzsche cell of the row on what is drawn from the will, for its »weshalb« clause, after the
review (below); the three notes extended; sentences added under *On what the will is* (what is
parallel to Schopenhauer's cells: nothing the map sets), under *On freedom* (what is parallel to
Kant: nothing the map sets; what is not: Kant's »frei« a predicate of a will as belonging to a thing
in itself, JGB 19's what is called by a name and what a word is the word for, saying nothing of a
thing in itself or the law of nature) and under *On what is drawn from the will* (a philosopher's
right over a doctrine beside Kant's place of morality; no passage joins the two »Moral«); the gaps
list, which now names the sentences of all four spans in no claim; the closing paragraph. README
counts 262 nodes (3 thinkers, 5 works, 77 passages, 157 claims, 14 arguments, 4 readings, 2
comparisons); the colophon agrees. No argument was formalized.

**The adversarial pass, and what it found.** A second agent was given the six new nodes, the diff
of the page and the README, the Gutenberg file, the older JGB 19 nodes and every Kant, Schopenhauer
and Nietzsche node the bodies cite or argue absences against, the conventions and the distilled
rules, with the brief to verify both originals by script and the four spans' concatenation, count

translation's length, every translation word by word (twenty-four points named), every statement's

- *Serious, a dead link the build did not catch.* The body of the last claim linked

## 2026-10-24 — run 18: GD »Die vier grossen Irrthümer« 7 and 8, the error of free will, and nobody responsible

Eighteenth scheduled run, on `main`, on the configured model `claude-fable-5-1` (the same the
last entry records; no handover). Read all governing files first. The run fired the day after
run 17; `origin/main` stood at run 17's commit when it began, and nothing arrived in between.
The container had `main` checked out, tracking `origin/main`.

**Step 1.** No ask changed status. Asks 4 (deferred), 9 and 10 stay open; none blocks the
plan.

**Step 2.** `INBOX.md` carries the owner notes of 2026-10-11 and 2026-10-19, each handled in
the session that received it; nothing new. No dispute, no attestation, no form filing (the
workflow made no commit).

**Step 3.** No open motion.

**Step 4, the nodes: 7 new, both comparisons touched, four claims added to one cell.** The
plan's next content item was a gap both pages list that adds a cell; the will page's row on
whether the will is free named GD »Die vier grossen Irrthümer« 7–8 and JGB 19 beyond its two
spans, and the two GD sections were taken, since section 7 is titled »Irrthum vom freien
Willen« and answers the row's question in its own words, where JGB 19's remainder is on the
will's complexity. The Gutenberg text (eBook 7203, the same transcription as every GD passage in
the map) was fetched, the two sections extracted by start and end substring with the line breaks
joined by one space, and asserted verbatim before anything was written.

*The passages.* `p-gd-irrthuemer-7-error-of-free-will`, section 7 whole, nine sentences
counting its title line, and `p-gd-irrthuemer-8-nobody-responsible`, section 8 whole, the last
of the chapter, eleven counting its opening question. Two forms of the transcription are kept
and named on the first passage: »überall« opening its sentence in lower case, which the
transcription prints elsewhere too, and »Willens-Psychiologie«, which the map reads as the
transcription's slip for the word the same sentence uses and keeps as transcribed, the printed
edition unchecked. Terms new to the map: »Theologen-Kunststück« "theologians' artifice",
»Unschuld des Werdens« "innocence of becoming", »Schuldig-finden-wollen« "wanting to find
guilty", »Falschmünzerei« "counterfeiting", »sittliche Weltordnung« "moral world-order",
»Befreiung« "liberation" (not »Freiheit«), »erlösen« "redeem"; and »ein Stück Verhängniss«
rendered "a piece of fate" where Vernunft 5's »Verhängniss von Irrthum« is "calamity", the
difference argued on the passage (»Fatalität« twice in the sentence before, »nothwendig« in the
sentence itself) rather than hidden.

*The five claims, all `draft`, each with its quote.* From section 7: (1) today »wir« have no
pity any more for the concept »"freier Wille"«, »das anrüchigste Theologen-Kunststück, das es
giebt«, for the purpose of making humanity »"verantwortlich"« in the theologians' sense, that
is, dependent on them; (2) becoming has been stripped of its innocence when any being-so-and-so
is traced back to will, intentions, acts of responsibility: »die Lehre vom Willen ist wesentlich
erfunden zum Zweck der Strafe, das heisst des Schuldig-finden-wollens«; (3) »Die Menschen wurden
"frei" gedacht, um gerichtet, um gestraft werden zu können«, »folglich« every action had to be
thought as willed and as originating in consciousness, the »folglich« inside one sentence, which
the map formalizes as no argument and sets no `depends_on` for, since it runs from the purpose
of a thought to what had to be thought. From section 8: (4) nobody gives man his qualities,
»noch er selbst«, the nonsense of that notion »als "intelligible Freiheit" von Kant, vielleicht
auch schon von Plato gelehrt worden«, the one sentence of the two sections that names Kant,
reported as Nietzsche's attribution; (5) that nobody is made responsible any more, that the kind
of being is not traced back to a »causa prima«, that the world is no unity, »dies erst ist die
grosse Befreiung«, and with it the innocence of becoming is restored. The other fifteen sentences
of the two sections are in the passages and in no claim, listed on the passages and in the will
page's gaps list. No link on any of the five: the texts join the sentences by no connective the
map could quote, and no attack is set. Argued on the nodes: no attack on Kant's B xxvii–xxviii
or on the antinomy's resolution, since (1) says what the concept is and what it is for and
denies no statement that a will is free, (3) says why men were thought free, and (4) denies that
man gives himself his »Eigenschaften«, which no Kant statement in the map asserts, the phrase
»intelligible Freiheit« being in no Kant passage the map holds (the nearest the »intelligibelen
Charakter« of A 539 / B 567); no attack on Schopenhauer's § 23, whose »frei« is freedom from
the forms, which none of the sentences speaks of; no link between (1) and JGB 21's »Unbegriff
"freier Wille"«, the same term three years earlier, since neither text names the other; and GD
8's »causa prima« is not identified with JGB 21's »causa sui«. The sentence on the will in
section 8, »Er ist nicht die Folge einer eignen Absicht, eines Willens, eines Zwecks«, is in no
claim and named on the page.

*The pages.* `x-the-will`: claims (1) to (4) added to the Nietzsche cell of the row on freedom
after JGB 21's six, the note extended, the two passages listed, a paragraph added under *On
freedom* (what is parallel to Kant, both texts' »gedacht«, said to be the map's reading; what is
not, Kant's »jenem nicht unterworfen« against GD's »"frei"« as the condition of being judged
and punished, responsibility before a judge, a sense of freedom no Kant passage in the map
names; the sentence naming Kant and why no attack is set; the close of the two sections on no
row), one sentence under *On what is drawn from the will* saying why neither section is set in
that row, the gaps list and the closing paragraph. `x-thing-in-itself`: the gaps list, which had
named GD 7–8 since 2026-10-12, now says they are in the map and set on the will page, and the
closing paragraph records the change. README counts 256 nodes (3 thinkers, 5 works, 75
passages, 153 claims, 14 arguments, 4 readings, 2 comparisons); the colophon agrees. No
argument was formalized: the sections state a genealogy and a doctrine and the one »folglich«
is inside a sentence.

**The adversarial pass, and what it found.** A second agent was given the seven new nodes, the diff of the three revised files, the
Gutenberg file, the Kant, Schopenhauer and Nietzsche nodes the new ones cite or argue absences
against, the conventions and the distilled rules, with the brief to verify both originals
character by character by script, count the sentences the passages and the gaps list assert,
check every quote and its translation, every translation word by word (»Theologen-Kunststück«,
»pflegt es«, »abwälzen«, »Stück Verhängniss«, »die hier zuletzt abgelehnten Vorstellung«, »Art
des Seins«, »damit erst« named as points to attack), every statement against its sentence's
qualifiers, every asserted absence by grepping the map, whether any attack link or `depends_on`
is due, the fit of each of the four cells to its row, the page prose, the gaps lists, the
provenance paragraphs, the format, the counts and the rendered pages, and to grep both pages and
